Duality + Reiterman! As shown in the proof of the Local Variety Theorem, a family
(\,W_{\Sigma}\subseteq{\mathsf{Rec}}_{\mathcal{F}}(\Sigma)\,)_{\Sigma\in\mathds{A}}
of local varieties corresponds via duality to a family of
\Sigma
-generated profinite
{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}_{\mathcal{F}}
-algebras in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
. By Lemma
128
, the family
(W_{\Sigma})_{\Sigma}
forms a variety of
languages (i.e. is closed under preimages) iff the dual family of

\Sigma
-generated profinite
{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}_{\mathcal{F}}
-algebras forms a
pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory. This shows that the lattice of varieties of languages over
\Sigma
is isomorphic to the lattice of pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories. Moreover, the Reiterman Theorem (see Theorem
58
) asserts that the latter is isomorphic to the lattice of pseudovarieties of
\mathcal{F}
-algebras. This proves the Variety Theorem.
∎

For concrete applications of our (Local) Variety Theorem, see Section
8
.

Remark 129
.

Straubing
[
42
]
studied
\mathsf{C}
-varieties of
regular languages
which are defined as Eilenberg’s varieties of
regular languages, except that closure under preimages is required
only w.r.t. a given class
\mathsf{C}
of monoid morphisms. By
making a class
\mathsf{C}
of
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphisms an
additional parameter of our framework, Theorem
127

easily generalizes to a monad version of Straubing’s variety theorem for
\mathsf{C}
-varieties. In more detail, let
\mathsf{C}
be a family associating to each pair
(\Sigma,\Delta)\in\mathds{A}^{2}
a set
\mathsf{C}(\Delta,\Sigma)
of
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphisms from

\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}
to
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. A
\mathsf{C}
-variety of languages
is
given as in Definition
126
, but with
g
restricted to
homomorphisms in
\mathsf{C}
. Similarly, a
pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory
and an
\mathcal{F}
-theory w.r.t.
\mathsf{C}
is
given as in Definition
43
and
44
, but with
g
again
restricted to homomorphisms in
\mathsf{C}
. This leads to the following

Theorem 130

(Straubing Theorem for
\mathbf{T}
-algebras)
.

The lattice of
\mathsf{C}
-varieties of languages is isomorphic to the lattice of

\mathcal{F}
-theories w.r.t.
\mathsf{C}
.

Proof.

This is in complete analogy to the first part of the proof of the Variety Theorem: a family of local varieties of languages forms a
\mathsf{C}
-variety iff its dual family of
\Sigma
-generated profinite
{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}_{\mathcal{F}}
-algebras forms a pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory w.r.t.
\mathsf{C}
. And by an argument analogous to Lemma
47
, pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories w.r.t.
\mathsf{C}
correspond to
\mathcal{F}
-theories w.r.t
\mathsf{C}
.
∎

The special case
\mathbf{T}=\mathbf{T}_{*}
on
\mathbf{Set}
is due to Straubing

[
42
]
.

7
Reduced
\mathbf{T}
-algebras and the Reduced Variety Theorem

We have seen that in a many-sorted setting, a restriction of the alphabets is often necessary to capture the proper languages. In some cases, it can is also be necessary to restrict the sorts. For example, recall that an
\infty
-language is a two-sorted subset of
T_{\infty}(\Sigma,\emptyset)=(\Sigma^{+},\Sigma^{\omega})
. If one is only interested in
\omega
-languages
(i.e. languages of infinite words), one needs restrict to subsets of
T_{\infty}(\Sigma,\emptyset)
that are empty in the first sort.

In this section, we present a variety theorem that is parametric in a subset
S_{0}\subseteq S
of the sorts and considers only recognizable languages that are empty outside of
S_{0}
. On the algebraic side, this means to restrict to
\mathbf{T}
-algebras that are
reduced
with respect to their
S_{0}
-components. As concrete applications of our Reduced Variety Theorem (see Theorem
183
), will derive Eilenberg-type correspondences for
\omega
-regular languages and regular tree languages in Section
8
.

The results of this section are inspired by the paper
[
39
]
where reduced languages and algebras are investigated for the special case of tree languages. They generalize the results of the previous sections, which emerge by taking
S_{0}=S
. Many arguments are straightforward adaptions, so we will only sketch some of the proofs and emphasize on the new concepts.

Notation 131
.

Throughout this section, we fix a subset
S_{0}\subseteq S
of sorts and continue to work with a fixed class
\mathds{A}\in\mathbf{Set}_{f}^{S}
of alphabets. Moreover, let us fix for each
\Sigma\in\mathds{A}
a unary presentation
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, and let
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
denote the closure of
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
under composition and identity morphisms
\mathit{id}\colon(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\to(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}
. Then also
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
forms a unary presentation of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. We write
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
for the set of all unary operations
u\colon(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\to(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{t}
in
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, where
s,t\in S
.

7.1
Reduced
\mathbf{T}
-algebras

In this subsection, we investigate
\mathbf{T}
-algebras that are “minimal” with respect to their
S_{0}
-part. They form the algebraic counterpart of languages that are empty in non-
S_{0}
sorts.

Definition 132
.

A
\mathbf{T}
-algebra
(A,\alpha)
is called
reduced
if there exists no proper quotient
e\colon(A,\alpha)\twoheadrightarrow(B,\beta)
in
\mathbf{Alg}\,{\mathbf{T}}
whose components
e_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A_{s}\twoheadrightarrow B_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
are isomorphisms. By a
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra
is meant a
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra
e\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow(A,\alpha)
whose codomain
(A,\alpha)
is reduced.

Remark 133
.

For
S=S_{0}
, every
\mathbf{T}
-algebra is reduced.

Definition 134
.

Let
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
be a quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
. Then
e
is called

1.

reduced
if there exists no proper quotient
e\colon A\twoheadrightarrow B
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
whose components
e_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}A_{s}\twoheadrightarrow B_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
are isomorphisms.

2.

a
reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
if it is (i) reduced, (ii)
\mathcal{F}
-refinable and (iii) every unary operation
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}(s,t)
with
s,t\in S_{0}
lifts along
e
, i.e. there exists
u_{A}\colon A_{s}\to A_{t}
with
e\cdot u=u_{A}\cdot e
.

\textstyle{(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{A_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{A}}
\textstyle{A_{t}}

Remark 135
.

A quotient
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
is reduced iff, for all sorts
s\not\in S_{0}
, the set
{|A_{s}|}
has at most one element. Therefore a reduced quotient
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
is completely determined by its components
e_{s}\colon(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\to A_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
.

Remark 136
.

In the special case
S_{0}=S
, the reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
are exactly the
\mathcal{F}
-quotients of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. Indeed, in this case the lifting property of
e
in the above definition states exactly that
e
is a
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
-quotient in the sense of Definition
65
, and thus corresponds to an
\mathcal{F}
-quotient because
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
is a unary presentation of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

Our goal in this subsection is to show that
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras correspond to reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
(see Lemma
142
). The key is the following construction:

Notation 137
.

Let
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
be a quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
.

1.

If
\mathscr{D}
is variety of algebras, the congruence
\equiv_{e}
on
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
is defined as follows: for any sort
s
and elements
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
, put

x\equiv_{e}x^{\prime}\quad\text{iff}\quad e_{t}\cdot u(x)=e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime})\text{ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}.

2.

If
\mathscr{D}
is variety of ordered algebras, the stable preorder
\preceq_{e}
on
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
is defined as follows: for any sort
s
and elements
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
, put

x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}\quad\text{iff}\quad e_{t}\cdot u(x)\leq e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime})\text{ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}.

We denote by
e_{R}\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A_{R}
the quotient in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
induced by
\equiv_{e}
(resp.
\preceq_{e}
), see
C
and
C
.

The following lemma collects the key properties of
e_{R}
:

Lemma 138
.

For any
\mathcal{F}
-refinable reduced quotient
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
, the following holds:

1.

e_{R}
is an
\mathcal{F}
-quotient, i.e. carries a
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra
e_{R}\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow(A_{R},\alpha_{R})
.

2.

e_{R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e\leq e_{R}
.

3.

(A_{R},\alpha_{R})
is reduced.

4.

If
e
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient, then
e_{R}
is the unique
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e_{R,s}=e_{s}
for all
s\in S_{0}
.

Proof.

We consider the case where
\mathscr{D}
is a variety of ordered algebras; for the unordered case, replace equations by inequations and stable preorders by congruences.

We first establish an auxiliary statement:

(
\ast
)
Claim.
If
h\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow B
is a
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e\leq h
then also
e_{R}\leq h
.

Proof.
Suppose that
e\leq h
, i.e.
e=p\cdot h
for some morphism
p\colon B\twoheadrightarrow A
. To show that
e_{R}\leq h
, we apply the homomorphism theorem. Thus suppose that elements
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
are given with
h(x)\leq h(x^{\prime})
. We need to show that
e_{R}(x)\leq e_{R}(x^{\prime})
, i.e.
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
. For any
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
with
t\in S_{0}
, we have

e_{t}\cdot u(x)=p_{t}\cdot h_{t}\cdot u(x)=p_{t}\cdot u_{B}\cdot h_{s}(x)\leq p_{t}\cdot u_{B}\cdot h_{s}(x^{\prime})=p_{t}\cdot h_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime})=e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime}).

Here
u_{B}
is the lifting of
u
along
h
, which exists because
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
is a unary presentation of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
and
h
is an
\mathcal{F}
-quotient. This shows
x\preceq_{s}x^{\prime}
, as claimed. Thus by the homomorphism theorem
e_{R}
factors through
h
, proving (
\ast
).

Now for the proof of the four statements in the lemma.

1.

Since the quotient
e
is
\mathcal{F}
-refinable by hypothesis,
e_{R}
is also
\mathcal{F}
-refinable by (
\ast
). To show that
e_{R}
is an
\mathcal{F}
-quotient (equivalently, that
\preceq_{e}
is an
\mathcal{F}
-congruence), it suffices to verify that
\preceq_{e}
is a
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-congruence, since
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
is a unary presentation. Thus suppose that
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
are given with
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
and let
u\in\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
. We need to show
u(x)\preceq_{e}u(x^{\prime})
. For all
v\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(t,t^{\prime})
with
t^{\prime}\in S_{0}
, we have
v\cdot u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}(s,t^{\prime})
by closure under composition, and thus
e_{t^{\prime}}\cdot v\cdot u(x)\leq e_{t^{\prime}}\cdot v\cdot u(x^{\prime})
since
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
. But this means precisely that
u(x)\preceq_{e}u(x^{\prime})
by the definition of
\preceq_{e}
, as desired.

2.

We first show that
e\leq e_{R}
, using the homomorphism theorem. Let
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
with
e_{R}(x)\leq e_{R}(x^{\prime})
(i.e.
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
). We need to show that
e_{s}(x)\leq e_{s}(x^{\prime})
. The latter is trivial for
s\not\in S_{0}
because
e
is reduced, i.e. the codomain of
e_{s}
has at most one element. For
s\in S_{0}
, we have
e_{t}\cdot u(x)\leq e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime})
for all
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
with
t\in S_{0}
by the definition of
\preceq_{e}
. In particular, putting
u=\mathit{id}_{(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}}
yields
e_{s}(x)\leq e_{s}(x^{\prime})
. Thus
e\leq e_{R}
by the homomorphism theorem.

That
e_{R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e\leq e_{R}
now follows from (
\ast
).

3.

Suppose that
q\colon(A_{R},\alpha_{R})\twoheadrightarrow(B,\beta)
is a quotient of
(A_{R},\alpha_{R})
such that
q_{s}
is an isomorphism for every
s\in S_{0}
. We need to show that
q
is an isomorphism. To this end, we first show that
e\leq q\cdot e_{R}
, using once again the homomorphism theorem: given
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
with
q\cdot e_{R}(x)\leq q\cdot e_{R}(x^{\prime})
, we need to show that
e(x)\leq e(x^{\prime})
. For
s\not\in S_{0}
this holds trivially because
e
is reduced, i.e. the codomain of
e_{s}
has at most one element. Thus let
s\in S_{0}
. Then
q\cdot e_{R}(x)\leq q\cdot e_{R}(x)
implies
e_{R}(x)\leq e_{R}(x^{\prime})
because
q_{s}
is an isomorphism, and thus
e(x)\leq e^{\prime}(x)
because
e\leq e_{R}
by part 2 of the lemma. Thus, the homomorphism theorem gives
e\leq q\cdot e_{R}
. By part 2 again, we conclude
e_{R}\leq q\cdot e_{R}
. On the other hand, we trivially have
q\cdot e_{R}\leq e_{R}
. Thus
q\cdot e_{R}\cong e_{R}
, which shows that
q
is an isomorphism.

4.

Suppose that
e
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient. We first show that
e_{R,s}=e_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
. To this end, it suffices to show that
e_{R,s}
and
e_{s}
have the same ordered kernel: for all
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
one has
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
iff
e_{s}(x)\leq e_{s}(x^{\prime})
. The “only if” direction follows from (
\ast
). For the “if” direction, let
e_{s}(x)\leq e_{s}(x^{\prime})
. To show that
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
, let
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}(s,t)
with
t\in S_{0}
. Then

e_{t}\cdot u(x)=u_{A}\cdot e_{s}(x)\leq u_{A}\cdot e_{s}(x^{\prime})=e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime}),

where
u_{A}
is the lifting of
u
along
e
. It exists because
s,t\in S_{0}
and
e
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient. Thus, by the definition of
\preceq_{e}
we have
x\preceq_{e}x^{\prime}
, as claimed.

For the uniqueness statement, suppose that
h\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow(B,\beta)
is a
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
h_{s}=e_{s}
for all
s\in S_{0}
. Then
e\leq h
; indeed, we have
e=p\cdot h
where
p\colon B\twoheadrightarrow A
is the morphism with
p_{s}=\mathit{id}_{A_{s}}
for
s\in S_{0}
, and otherwise the unique morphism into
A_{s}
. Thus, by (
\ast
), we also have
e_{R}\leq h
, i.e.
e_{R}=q\cdot h
for some
q\colon B\twoheadrightarrow A_{R}
. Clearly
q_{s}=\mathit{id}
for
s\in S_{0}
because
h_{s}=e_{R,s}=e_{s}
. Since
(B,\beta)
is reduced, it follows that
q
is an isomorphism. Thus
h\cong e_{R}
.
∎

Lemma 139
.

Let
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
be two reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

1.

The subdirect product
e
of
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient, and moreover

2.

e_{R}
is the subdirect product of
e_{0,R}
and
e_{1,R}
in
\mathbf{Alg}\,{\mathbf{T}}
.

3.

The pushout of
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient.

Proof.

1.

By the definition of the subdirect product (see
A
), clearly
e
is a reduced quotient. Next, we show that
e
is
\mathcal{F}
-refinable. Since
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
are
\mathcal{F}
-refinable, they factor through some (w.l.o.g. the same)
\mathcal{F}
-quotient
h
of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, i.e.
e_{0},e_{1}\leq h
. But since
e
is the smallest quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
above
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
, we also have
e\leq h
, showing that
e
is
\mathcal{F}
-refinable. That
e
satisfies the lifting property is shown as in the proof of Lemma
81
.1.

2.

We need to prove that
e_{R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e_{i,R}\leq e_{R}
(
i=0,1
). First, by Lemma
138
.2 we have
e\leq e_{R}
and thus also
e_{i}\leq e_{R}
, since
e_{i}\leq e
. By Lemma
138
.2 again,
e_{i,R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e_{i}\leq e_{i,R}
, and therefore
e_{i,R}\leq e_{R}
. In remains to show that
e_{R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra satisfying the latter inequality. Thus let
q
be any
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e_{i,R}\leq q
for
i=0,1
. Since
e_{i}\leq e_{i,R}
, we get
e_{i}\leq q
for
i=0,1
, and thus
e\leq q
by the minimality of
e
. But since
e_{R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e\leq e_{R}
, it follows that
e_{R}\leq q
, as desired.

3.

Let
p
be the pushout of
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
. Clearly
p
is reduced and
\mathcal{F}
-refinable because
e_{0}
is and
p\leq e_{0}
. The lifting property is established as in the proof of Lemma
81
.2.∎

Notation 140
.

For any morphism
h\colon A\to B
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
, we denote by
h_{\downarrow}\colon A\to B_{\downarrow}
the morphism where (i)
h_{\downarrow,s}=h_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
and (ii)
h_{\downarrow,s}
is the image of the unique morphism
A_{s}\to 1
for
s\not\in S_{0}
. In particular,
B_{\downarrow,s}=B_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
, and
B_{\downarrow,s}
has at most one element for
s\not\in S_{0}
. Moreover, if
h
is a quotient, then
h_{\downarrow}
is a reduced quotient in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
.

Remark 141
.

If
h\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow(A,\alpha)
is a
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra, then
h_{\downarrow}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. Indeed, that
h_{\downarrow}
satisfies the lifting property of Definition
134
.2 follows immediately from the fact that
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
forms a unary presentation of
\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
and
h
is an
\mathcal{F}
-quotient.

Lemma 142
.

The maps

h\mapsto h_{\downarrow}\quad\text{and}\quad e\mapsto e_{R}

define an isomorphism between the poset of
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras and the poset of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

Proof.

(a)

For every reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
, we have
e=e_{R,\downarrow}
. Indeed, clearly both quotients agree on sorts
s\not\in S_{0}
because they are reduced. On sorts
s\in S_{0}
, the quotient
e
agrees with
e_{R}
by Lemma
138
.4, and
e_{R}
agrees with
e_{R,\downarrow}
by the definition of
(\mathord{-})_{\downarrow}
.

(b)

For every
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra
h\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow(A,\alpha)
, we have
h=h_{\downarrow,R}
. Indeed, since both
h
and
h_{\downarrow,R}
agree with the reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
e\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=h_{\downarrow}
on sorts
s\in S_{0}
, this follows from Lemma
138
.4.

(c)

It remains to show that the two maps
h\mapsto h_{\downarrow}
and
e\mapsto e_{R}
are monontone. Clearly the map
h\mapsto h_{\downarrow}
is monotone. For the monotonicity of
e\mapsto e_{R}
, suppose that
e_{i}\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A_{i}
,
i=0,1
, are reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients with
e_{0}\leq e_{1}
, i.e.
e_{0}
factorizes through
e_{1}
. Since
e_{1}\leq e_{1,R}
by Lemma
138
.2, we also have
e_{0}\leq e_{1,R}
. But since (again by Lemma
138
.2)
e_{0,R}
is the smallest
\Sigma
-generated
\mathcal{F}
-algebra with
e_{0}\leq e_{0,R}
, it follows that
e_{0,R}\leq e_{1,R}
.∎

With the help of unary presentations, finite reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras can be described as follows:

Lemma 143
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathds{A}
-generated algebra and let
e\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
be a surjective
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphism with
\Sigma\in\mathds{A}
. Then the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

A
is reduced.

(ii)

For each sort
s\not\in S_{0}
and any two elements
a,a^{\prime}\in{|A_{s}|}
,

\text{if $u_{A}(a)=u_{A}(a^{\prime})$ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}\qquad\text{then}\qquad a=a^{\prime},

and in the ordered case

\text{if $u_{A}(a)\leq u_{A}(a^{\prime})$ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}\qquad\text{then}\qquad a\leq a^{\prime},

where
u_{A}
is the lifting of
u
along
e
.

Proof.

We consider the case where
\mathscr{D}
is a variety of ordered algebras. That
A
is reduced means precisely that
e=e_{\downarrow,R}
by Lemma
142
. Since
e_{\downarrow,R}\leq e
by Lemma
138
.2, the latter equation is equivalent to
e\leq e_{\downarrow,R}
. By the homomorphism theorem, this is the case precisely if, for all
x,x^{\prime}\in{|T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}|}_{s}
,

x\preceq_{e_{\downarrow}}x^{\prime}\quad\text{implies}\quad e(x)\leq e(x^{\prime})

i.e.

\text{if $e_{t}\cdot u(x)\leq e_{t}\cdot u(x^{\prime})$ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}\qquad\text{then}\qquad e(x)\leq e(x^{\prime}).

For
s\in S_{0}
this implication is trivially satisfied (take
u=\mathit{id}_{(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}}
). Thus it suffices to restrict to sorts
s\not\in S_{0}
. But then the above implication means exactly that, for
a,a^{\prime}\in{|A_{s}|}
with
s\in S_{0}
,

\text{if $u_{A}(a)\leq u_{A}(a^{\prime})$ for all $u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)$ with $t\in S_{0}$}\qquad\text{then}\qquad a\leq a^{\prime},

because
e
is surjective and
u_{A}
is the lifting of
u
along
e
.
∎

Example 144
.

Let
\mathbf{T}=\mathbf{T}_{\infty}
on
\mathbf{Set}^{S}
with
S=\{+,\omega\}
,
S_{0}=\{\omega\}
,
\mathds{A}=\{(\Sigma,\emptyset)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Sigma\in\mathbf{Set}_{f}\}
,
\mathcal{F}=
all finite
\omega
-semigroups. Given an
\mathds{A}
-generated (= complete) finite
\omega
-semigroup
A
, express
A
as a quotient
e\colon(\Sigma^{+},\Sigma^{\omega})\twoheadrightarrow(A_{+},A_{\omega})
with
\Sigma\in\mathbf{Set}_{f}
. For the unary presentation
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
of
(\Sigma^{+},\Sigma^{\omega})
as in Example
75
, the unary operations in
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(+,\omega)
(i.e. from
\Sigma^{+}
to
\Sigma^{\omega}
) have the form
x\mapsto yxz
and
x\mapsto y(xy^{\prime})^{\omega}
with
y,y^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{*}
and
z\in\Sigma^{\omega}
. The corresponding lifted operations from
A_{+}
to
A^{\omega}
have the form
a\mapsto bac
and
a\mapsto b(ab^{\prime})^{\omega}
with
b,b^{\prime}\in A_{+}\cup\{1\}
and
c\in A_{\omega}
. Thus, by the above lemma the
\omega
-semigroup
A
is reduced iff, for all
a,a^{\prime}\in A_{+}
,

\text{if $bac=ba^{\prime}c$ and $b(ab^{\prime})^{\omega}=b(a^{\prime}b^{\prime})^{\omega}$ for all $b,b^{\prime}\in A_{+}\cup\{1\}$ and $c\in A_{\omega}$}\qquad\text{then}\qquad a=a^{\prime}.

In other words,
A
is reduced iff any two distinct elements of
A_{+}
can be separated by some iterated unary operation from
A_{+}
into
A_{\omega}
.

7.2
Local pseudovarieties of reduced
\mathbf{T}
-algebras

In this section we consider reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
and show that they correspond to local pseudovarieties of
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras. This is the reduced analogue of the Local Reiterman Theorem
31
.

Notation 145
.

Recall that we denote the continuous extension of
u\in\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
by
\hat{u}\colon(\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\to(\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{t}
, see Lemma
83
. Then every
u=u_{n}\cdot\ldots\cdot u_{1}
in
\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}
has the continuous extension
\hat{u}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\hat{u}_{n}\cdot\ldots\cdot\hat{u}_{1}
.
Moreover, the continuous extension of an
\mathcal{F}
-refinable quotient
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathscr{D}^{S}
is denoted by
\hat{e}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
(see Lemma
82
).

The following definition gives the analogous concepts of Definition
134
for
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
:

Definition 146
.

Let
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
be a quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
. Then
\varphi
is called

1.

reduced
if there exists no proper quotient
\varphi\colon P\twoheadrightarrow P^{\prime}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
whose components
\varphi_{s}
for
s\in S_{0}
are isomorphisms.

2.

a
reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
if it is (i) reduced and (ii) for every unary operation
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
with
s,t\in S_{0}
, the continuous extension
\hat{u}
lifts along
\varphi
, i.e. there exists
u_{P}\colon P_{s}\to P_{t}
with
\varphi\cdot\hat{u}=u_{P}\cdot\varphi
.

\textstyle{(\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{u}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{(\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}})_{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{P_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{P}}
\textstyle{P_{s}}

Remark 147
.

If
S_{0}=S
, a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
corresponds precisely to a
\Sigma
-generated profinite
{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}_{\mathcal{F}}
-algebra, see Lemma
87
.

Remark 148
.

In analogy to Remark
85
, if the object
P
in the above definition is finite, then
\varphi
forms a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
iff its restriction
V\varphi\cdot\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
forms a
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

Definition 149
.

A
local pseudovariety of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients
is an ideal in the poset of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
(cf. Definition
25
).

We aim to show that local pseudovarieties of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients correspond to reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. The following construction gives the translation between the two concepts, generalizing Construction
28
.

Construction 150
.

1.

To every reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
we associate the class
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
of all quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
of the form

e=(\,T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}}V\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{V\varphi}P\xrightarrow{Ve^{\prime}}A\,),

where
e^{\prime}\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A
is a finite quotient in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
and
e^{\prime}\cdot\varphi
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
. Note that any such morphism
e
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
: it is clearly reduced, it is
\mathcal{F}
-refinable by Lemma
82
, and satisfies the lifting property by Remark
148
.

2.

To every local pseudovariety
\mathscr{P}
of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients we associate a quotient

\varphi_{\mathscr{P}}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P^{\mathscr{P}}

in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
as follows. View
\mathscr{P}
as a full subcategory of the comma category
(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\downarrow\mathscr{D}_{f})
and form the cofiltered limit
P^{\mathscr{P}}
of the diagram

\mathscr{P}\mapsto\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S},\quad(e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A)\mapsto A,

with limit projections denoted by

e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A.

Note that the projections are surjective by Lemma
B.2
. The morphisms
\hat{e}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
(where
e
ranges over elements of
\mathscr{P}
) form a compatible family over the same diagram, and thus there exists a unique
\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P^{\mathscr{P}}
making the triangle below commute:

\textstyle{\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{e}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}}
\textstyle{P^{\mathscr{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}}
\textstyle{A}

The morphism
\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}
is surjective by Lemma
B.1
.

Lemma 151
.

1.

For every reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
, the class
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
forms a local pseudovariety of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

2.

For every local pseudovariety
\mathscr{P}
of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, the morphism
\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

Proof.

1.

Let
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
be a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient. Clearly
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
is downwards closed. To show that
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
is directed, let

e_{i}=(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}}V\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{V\varphi}P\xrightarrow{Ve_{i}^{\prime}}A_{i}),\quad i=0,1,

be two elements of
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
. We need to find an element
e
with
e_{0},e_{1}\leq e
. Form the subdirect product
e^{\prime}\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A
of
e_{0}^{\prime}
and
e_{1}^{\prime}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
. Then
e^{\prime}\cdot\varphi
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient; the argument is completely analogous to the proof of Lemma
81
.1. Therefore

e\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}}V\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{V\varphi}P\xrightarrow{Ve^{\prime}}A),\quad i=0,1,

lies in
\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
, and moreover
e_{i}\leq e
because
e_{i}^{\prime}\leq e^{\prime}
.

2.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a local pseudovariety of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, and let
u\in\overline{\mathds{U}}_{\Sigma}(s,t)
with
s,t\in S_{0}
. We need to show that
\hat{u}
has a lifting along
\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}
. To this end, observe that the morphisms
P_{s}\xrightarrow{e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}}A_{s}\xrightarrow{u_{A}}A_{t}
(where
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
ranges over
\mathscr{P}
and
u_{A}
is the lifting of
u
along
e
) form a compatible family over the diagram defining the limit
P_{t}
. Therefore there exists a unique
u_{P}\colon P_{s}\to P_{t}
with
e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}\cdot u_{P}=u_{A}\cdot e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}
for all
e\colon T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathscr{P}
. This implies
u_{P}\cdot\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}=\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}\cdot\hat{u}
, as this equation holds when precomposed with the dense map
\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
and postcomposed with the jointly monomorphic limit projections
e_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}
.∎

Lemma 152
.

For any local pseudovariety
\mathscr{P}
of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, we have

\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
where
\varphi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}
.

\mathscr{P}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}

Lemma 153
.

Let
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
be two reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
.

1.

The subdirect product of
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient.

2.

The pushout of
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient.

3.

Given a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
and a finite quotient
e\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A
, there exists a finite quotient
h\colon P\twoheadrightarrow B
such that
h\cdot\varphi
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}D
and
e\leq h
.

\textstyle{\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A}

4.

Given a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
and a finite quotient
e\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A
, let
\mathscr{S}
be the full subcategory of
(P\downarrow\widehat{\mathscr{D}}_{f}^{S})
on all finite quotients
e\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A
such that
e\cdot\varphi
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient. Then
P
is the cofiltered limit of the diagram

\pi\colon\mathscr{S}\to\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S},\quad(e\colon P\twoheadrightarrow A)\mapsto A,

with limit projections
e
.

Proof.

The first two statements follow as in proof of Lemma
139
. The last two statements follow as in the proof of Lemma
86
.
∎

Lemma 154
.

For each reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient
\varphi\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P
, we have
\varphi=\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}
where
\mathscr{P}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
.

Proof.

Consider the diagram
\pi
as in Lemma
153
.3. Then
\mathscr{S}\cong\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}
, and thus by the definition of
P^{\mathscr{P}}
(see Construction
150
) the object
P^{\mathscr{P}}
is the limit of the diagram
\pi
. On the other hand, by Lemma
153
.4 also the object
P
is the limit of the diagram
\pi
with limit projections
e
. The uniqueness of limits gives an isomorphism
j\colon P\xrightarrow{\cong}P^{\mathscr{P}}
, and as in the proof of Lemma
30
this implies
\varphi\cong\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}
.
∎

Lemma 155
.

The maps

\varphi\mapsto\mathscr{P}^{\varphi}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathscr{P}\mapsto\varphi^{\mathscr{P}}

give an isomorphism between the lattice of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
and the lattice of pseudovarieties of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients.

Proof.

The two maps give mutually inverse bijections by Lemma
152
and
154
. That they are order-preserving is shown in analogy to the proof of Theorem
31
).
∎

Let us rephrase the above lemma in terms of reduced algebras:

Definition 156
.

By a
local pseudovariety of
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras
is meant a filter in the poset of
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras.

From the above lemma and Lemma
142
we get

Theorem 157

(Local Reiterman Theorem for Reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras)
.

The lattice of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
is isomorphic to the lattice of pseudovarieties of
\Sigma
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras.

7.3
Pseudovarieties of reduced
\mathbf{T}
-algebras

In this subsection, we consider pseudovarieties of reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras and show that they correspond to reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories. This is the reduced version of the Reiterman Theorem
58
. We continue to work with a fixed class
\mathds{A}\subseteq\mathbf{Set}_{f}^{S}
of alphabets.

Definition 158
.

We say that a
\mathbf{T}
-algebra
A

divides
a
\mathbf{T}
-algebra
B
if
A
is a quotient of a subalgebra of
B
, i.e. there exist
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphisms
e
and
m
as shown below:

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{A}

A
pseudovariety of reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras
is a full subcategory
\mathscr{V}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
such that all elements of
\mathscr{V}
are
\mathds{A}
-generated and reduced, and
\mathscr{V}
is closed under division of finite products. That is, whenever
A_{1},\ldots A_{n}\in\mathscr{V}
and
A
is an
\mathds{A}
-generated reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebra that divides
A_{1}\times\ldots\times A_{n}
, then
A\in\mathscr{V}
.

Remark 159
.

For
S=S_{0}
, every
\mathbf{T}
-algebra is reduced and thus a pseudovariety of reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras is just a pseudovariety of
\mathcal{F}
-algebras, see Definition
39
.

Definition 160
.

A
reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory
is a family

\varrho=(\,\varrho_{\Sigma}\colon\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P_{\Sigma}\,)_{\Sigma\in\mathds{A}}

such that
\varrho_{\Sigma}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
, and for
every

\mathbf{T}
-homomorphism
g\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\to\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
with

\Sigma,\Delta\in\mathds{A}
there exists a
morphism
g_{P}\colon P_{\Delta}\to P_{\Sigma}
with
\varrho_{\Sigma}\cdot\hat{g}=g_{P}\cdot\varrho_{\Delta}
.

\textstyle{\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{g}}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{\Delta}}
\textstyle{\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{P_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{P}}
\textstyle{P_{\Sigma}}

Here
\hat{g}
is the continuous extension of
g
, see Lemma
18
. Reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories are ordered by
\varrho\leq\varrho^{\prime}
iff

\varrho_{\Sigma}\leq\varrho_{\Sigma}^{\prime}
for each
\Sigma\in\mathds{A}
.

Using the local Reiterman theorem for reduced
\mathcal{F}
-algebras, pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories can be equivalently described in terms of local pseudovarieties:

Definition 161
.

A
reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theory
is a family
\mathcal{T}=(\,\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}\,)_{\Sigma\in\mathds{A}}
such that (i)
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}
is a local pseudovariety of reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
for every
\Sigma\in\mathds{A}
, and (ii) for every
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphism
g\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\to\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
with
\Sigma,\Delta\in\mathds{A}
and every
e\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}
, there exists an
\overline{e}\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\twoheadrightarrow B
in
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}
and a morphism
\overline{g}\colon B\to A
with
\overline{g}\cdot\overline{e}=e\cdot g
.

\textstyle{\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\overline{e}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{g}}
\textstyle{A}

Reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theories are ordered by
\mathcal{T}\leq\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
iff
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\prime}
for all
\Sigma\in\mathds{A}
.

Notation 162
.

1.

For any reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory
\varrho
, form the family

\mathcal{T}^{\varrho}=(\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\varrho})_{\Sigma\in\mathds{A}}

where
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\varrho}
is the local pseudovariety of
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotients of
\mathcal{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
associated to
\varrho_{\Sigma}
(see Construction
150
.1).

2.

For any reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theory
\mathcal{T}
, form the family

\varrho^{\mathcal{T}}=(\varrho_{\Sigma}^{\mathcal{T}}\colon{\widehat{\mathbf{T}}}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow P_{\Sigma}^{\mathcal{T}})

where
\varrho_{\Sigma}^{\mathcal{T}}
is the reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient of
\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
associated to
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}
(see Construction
150
.2).

In analogy to Lemma
47
we get

Lemma 163
.

1.

For any reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory
\varrho
, the family
\mathcal{T}^{\varrho}
forms a reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theory.

2.

For any reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theory
\mathcal{T}
, the family
\varrho^{\mathcal{T}}
forms a reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory.

3.

The maps
\varrho\mapsto\mathcal{T}^{\varrho}
and
\mathcal{T}\mapsto\varrho^{\mathcal{T}}
give an isomorphism between the poset of reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theories and the poset of reduced
\mathcal{F}
-theories.

Proof.

1.

Suppose that
\varrho
is a reduced pro-
\mathcal{F}
theory. Let
g\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\to\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}
be a
\mathbf{T}
-homomorphism with
\Sigma,\Delta\in\mathds{A}
, and let
e\colon\mathbf{T}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\varrho}
. By the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\Sigma}^{\varrho}
, the quotient
e
has the form

e=(T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\mathbb{\Sigma}}}V\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\xrightarrow{V\varrho_{\Sigma}}VP_{\Sigma}\xrightarrow{Vq}A)

for some
q
in
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{S}
. Factorize the morphism
q\cdot g_{P}
into a surjective morphism
\overline{q}\colon P_{\Delta}\to C
followed by an injective/order-reflecting morphism
\overline{g}\colon C\to A
. By Lemma
153
.3, there exists a finite quotient
h\colon P_{\Delta}\twoheadrightarrow C
such that
h\cdot\varrho_{\Delta}
is a reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient and
\overline{q}\leq h
. Then the reduced
\mathds{U}_{\Sigma}
-quotient

\overline{e}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(T{\mathbb{\Delta}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\mathbb{\Delta}}}V\hat{T}_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbb{\Delta}}\xrightarrow{V\varrho_{\Delta}}VP_{\Delta}\xrightarrow{Vh}C)

lies in
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}^{\varrho}
(by the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}^{\varrho}
) and
e\cdot g
factors through
\overline{e}
, as shown by the diagram below:

\textstyle{T{\mathbb{\Delta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{e}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\mathbb{\Delta}}}
\textstyle{T{\mathbb{\Sigma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

This shows that
\mathcal{T}^{\varrho}

\mathcal{F}
